            1. Introduction

                              TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY    P | 5

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

Introduction

Azadegan South Oil Field is situated in West Karoun province on a large long-axis anticline with N-S trending with

approximate dimensions about 20 km width and 60 km length near to Iran-Iraq border, to the east of Yaran Oil Field

(70 km. west of Ahwaz city).

The trap area of the south Azadegan is limited to 43.2x17.2 km. The Azadegan Oil field was discovered in 1999,

when Well AZN-1 was drilled vertically down to the Cretaceous Fahliyan Formation with total depth of 4,643m. The

results of this well, revealed the reservoir characteristics of the field. Following to the result of the well no. AZN-1,

another 5 exploration wells was drilled in different parts of this huge field down to Fahliyan Formation. In the later

stage the well No.10 assumed as exploration well for evaluation of deeper formations. This well was drilled in 2008,

down to the depth of 5340m (Neyriz Fm.), mainly to evaluate the Jurassic formations.

Based on the exploration wells results, carbonate reservoir of Sarvak Formation is broadly developed in Azadegan

Field. This formation was penetrated in all of seven exploration wells (AZN-1, AZN-2, AZN-3, AZN-4, AZN-5, AZN-

6 and AZN-10). Clastic oil-bearing zones of Kazhdumi Formation developed in main anticline of Azadegan Field,

extending from north to center of South Azadegan. The Clastic oil-bearing zones of Gadvan Formation developed

only in crest area and south parts of South Azadegan Filed, which was discovered by AZN-1 in crest and AZN-4 in

south parts. The carbonates of Fahliyan Formation also developed in Azadegan field, but the oil reservoir of this

formation developed in South part of South Azadegan.

The Azadegan Field is an elongated anticline, trending in N-S direction, belonging to "Arabian Trend".

The stratigraphy sequence of the field includes AghaJari, Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak,

Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan Formations, from top to bottom. This sequence in well no AZN-10, the

deepest up to now drilled well of the field, continues with Garau, Gotnia, Najmah, Sargalu, Alan, Mus and Neyriz

formations.

The Dominant lithofacies of Sarvak Formation are Rudist-bearing packstone and partially grainstone, which contains

abundant bioclasts. Some other facies like matrix-dominated wackestone and mudstone are also found.

                                   TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY    P | 6

      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

The AZADEGAN Oilfield is Located in Khuzestan Province and is Approximately Located 80 Kilometers West
of Ahwaz City, near to Iran-Iraq Border. It is in The Northwest of The YADAVARAN Field and in The West of
JUFEYR Field. The Average Monthly Temperature is 45ºc in summer, and Highest Temperature is 52ºc. The
Lowest Temperature is -4.4ºc in winter. Maximum Rainfall is 78 mm in February, Winter Season.

                                   TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY    P | 7

                 South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                    Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

 Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

F10, which is a Fahliyan producer as a Vertical well, is planned to be drilled on Pad-64, is located AZNS-046
and AZNS-052 wells in the Middle part of South Azadegan oil field in Area-II.

As the Previous exploration/delineation drilling in Azadegan oil field has proved presence of a very prolific
hydrocarbon bearing sedimentary column,  particularly within the Cretaceous sequences. The Fahliyan
Formation was divided into 6 zones, named as Fahliyan top, Fah-1, Fah-2, Fah-3, Fah-4 and Fah-5.

This well is planned to be drilled as a Vertical well, to Fahliyan Formation for logging and evaluating the
formation.

 Figure 1 illustrates F10 Vertical well location (Pad-64) on top of Fahliyan Formations and near AZNS-050,
AZNS-046, AZNS-110 and AZNS-052.

  Figure 2 shows the position of F10 target well on Pad-64. The main objective of this well is to provide the
required  data until base of Fahliyan formation and maximum exposure from the pay zone.

2.2  Reservoir Information

  2.2.1 Key Reservoir Descriptions

  Fahliyan Formation: This carbonate to argillaceous carbonate Formation is limited by overlying thick
  argillaceous successions in the upper part (eaquivalent to the Iraqi Ratawi Formation) and the main part
  consists of different bioclastic limestones with some layers of shale and argillaceous facies that can be
  assumed equivalent with Iraqi Yamama Formation. However in South Azadegan wells-1, 2 and 5 the
  Fahliyan Formation is characterized by fossiliferous limestone beds and interbedded shales. It should be
  noted that most of Majnoon wells penetrated into the Sarvak and Fahliyan carbonates as one of the main
  reservoir targets.

    2.3.1  Expected Stratigraphy and Formation Top in F10:

The area is completely covered by the alluvium of Holocene age; the drilled will be Aghajari, Gachsaran, Asmari,

Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan formations. A brief stratigraphic

sequence has been predicted as follows :

     -   Aghajari Formation (surface to 1085.3mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 1075.7 m)
The Aghajari Formation consists of a repetition of brown to grey calcareous sandstones, chert debris and gypsum-

veined red marl. A fauna of brackish to freshwater was observed, together with more marine fauna.. Aghajari

overlays Gachsaran Formation with a conformable boundary.

     -  Gachsaran Formation (1085.3-1439.3 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 354m)
The Gachsaran Formation with a thickness ranges from 400 m to 500 m thickens towards the north. Deposition of

the Gachsaran Formation is cyclic. The cycle began with a deposition of shallow-water marine carbonates, and then

it was overlain by a thick evaporitic phase. The evaporates were capped by red to variegated marl, which

represented the end of cycle. Gachsaran is laid on Asmari Formation with a comformable boundary.

     -   Asmari Formation (1439.3-1712.9 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 273.6 m)
The Asmari Formation in Azadegan Field developed a massive sandstone interval, with a few thin argillaceous

limestones in the upper sections. Sandstone are clear, fine to medium-sorted, and poorly consolidated. On the other

hand, the lower sections consist of intercalation of sandy, chalky, slightly dolomitic high-energy limestone with

sandstone layers. Asmari is parallel to Pabdeh Formation at the bottom.

     -  Pabdeh Formation (1712.5-2204.8 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 491.9 m)
Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field. The upper part is made of grey to dark-brown

argillaceous limestones and marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in Oligocene. The lower part

is composed of dark-brown, slightly argillaceous limestone. Pelagic fauna, mostly Globigerina and Globorotalia,

indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to late Eocene in age. It seems that no early Paleocene

Formation exists in the Azadegan area. Chert and cherty limestone layers at the bottom of Pabdeh Formation are

very important for drilling parameters on the PDC bit (as a notification for drilling operation department).

     -   Gurpi Formation (2204.8-2558.9mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 354.1 m)

                                   TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY   P | 19

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

Gurpi Formation consists of argillaceous limestone with higher clay content rather than Pabdeh Formation. About

50 meters of pure limestone will be observed in the middle part of the formation which divides it into 3 different parts

as below:

     -  Upper Gurpi (2204.8-2351.6mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 146.8 m)
The Gurpi Formation in the Azadegan Field consists of grey to dark-brownish, slightly argillaceous micritic limestone

with Maestrichtian to Campanian aged fauna. At Well AZN-01, about 50 meters of chalky limestones were

encountered in the middle of the Gurpi formation, which contains abundant fragments of rudists, bryozoa, corals,

and Orbitoides.

     -   Tarbur Member (2351.6-2424.1 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 72.5 m)
In the western Abadan Plain about 50 meters thick chalky limestone was encountered in the middle part of the Gurpi

Formation, which contains abundant fragments of bioclasts, suggesting very shallow depositional environment. This

interval is equivalnt to the Tarbur Formation with reservoir quality in other regions.

     -  Lower Gurpi (2424.1-2558.9 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 134.8 m)
This member consists of gray, light gray, medium hard argillaceous Limestone. The Gurpi formation is overlain

directly on Ilam formation by conformity contact.

     -   Ilam Formation (2558.9-2640.5 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 81.6 m)
In the Azadegan Field, the Ilam Formation consists of grey micritic limestone, often argillaceous, rarely pyritic,

limestones. Microfauna, mostly pelagic, including rotalids and oligostegina assemblage, a few algae, bryozoa, and

rudist fragments exist.

     -   Laffan Member (2640.5-2647.2mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 6.7 m)
The Laffan Formation developed a thin greenish-grey pyritic shale layer with ostracods fauna that capping the Sarvak

Formation.

     -   Sarvak Formation (2647.2-3292.7 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 645.5 m)
The Sarvak Formation developed a carbonate unit, which was gradually overlying the Kazhdumi Formation, and it

is abruptly terminated by an erosion phase, covered by a various late Cretaceous sediment. The lithology of Sarvak

consists of the dark grey fine grained nodular argillaceous limestone interbedded with thin marls in the bottom. The

Limestone graded upward into a white-to-buff massive chalky limestone with siliceous nodules; developed a very

massive tan-to-brown limestone containing abundant rudist debris in the middle part. In the upper part is made up

                                   TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY   P | 20

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

of rubbly limestone with red staining and leached zones of breccia and the top of Sarvak Formation is very

ferruginous.

     -  Kazhdumi Formation (3292.7-3488 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 195.3 m)
Kazhdumi Formation begins with dark brown, soft to medium hard, some fissile and calcareous Shale. This formation

is start with dark brown to dark green gray, medium hard to hard, soft to medium, high bituminous, calcareous Shale

interbedded with argillaceous limestone, sandy claystone, claystone, marl, sandy limestone, limestone beds. The

Argillaceous Limestone is dark brown to brown gray, medium hard to hard, micro bituminous. The Sandy claystone

is light green gray, soft, high calcareous. The Claystone is gray to light gray, soft. The Marl is green gray, soft, sticky.

The Sandy Limestone is dark green gray, hard, faint sandy. The Limestone is light gray to green gray, medium hard

to soft, partly argillaceous. The Kazhdumi Formation at base is consisting of Azadegan Member.

     -   Dariyan Formation (3488-3649.2 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 161.2 m)
In Dariyan Formation a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales and sandstones

were deposited in various environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is composed of low-energy, but

not very deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone with echinoids and algal fragments. Two sandstone units were

defined in the Gadvan Formation lateral changes and especially discontinuity of the sandstone layers which are

consist of fine clastics (shales and siltstones) may be expected

The Gadvan formation divided to three part:Dariyan formation starts with white to white cream grey clean limestone

type I/II, I. Base of Dariyan formation, appears grey, green Pyritic shale interbedded with argillaceous limestone.
This zone called transition zone and thickness is 16m. after last grey limestone indicates of Gadvan formation .

This carbonate formation contains three zones. D1 and D3 are non-reservoirs and D2 is a moderately poor reservoir.

     -  Gadvan Formation (3649.2-3942.6 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 293.4 m)
In This Formation a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales and sandstones were

deposited in various environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is composed of low-energy, but not very

deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone with echinoids and algal fragments. Two sandstone units were defined

in the Gadvan Formation lateral changes and especially discontinuity of the sandstone layers which are consist of

fine clastics (shales and siltstones) may be expected

The Gadvan Formation divided to three part;

Upper Gadvan; the upper part starts with occurrence of a terrigenous rock. The lithology is composed of grey

claystone and some shale.

Khalij Mbr.; appears with pure limestone, cream, light brown and in some intervals oil stain.

Lower Gadvan; consisting of alternating of marl, argillaceous limestone, limestone, claystone, sandy claystone and

sandstone beds. The Marl is green gray, soft, sticky, slightly calcareous. The Argillaceous Limestone is gray white

gray medium hard to soft, partly pyritic. The Limestone is white, white to light gray, hard. The Claystone is light gray,

soft, sticky. The Sandy Claystone is light gray, soft, sticky, fine sand grained. The Sandstone is brown to gray, partly

white buff, gray, friable, sorted, sub rounded, medium quartz grained, partly calcareous. This sandstone which occur

in Zone-3 and Zone-5 are the reservoir of this formation and in this part is equivalent with Kushk member.

     -   Fahliyan Formation (3942.6-4199.0 mMD)(TD) (Drilled Thickness: 256.4m +)
This formation is start with light gray, light green gray, soft Limestone interbedded with green gray, soft, sticky Marl,

green gray, light gray, soft, Argillaceous Limestone and dark green gray, soft, sticky Clay stone beds. This formation

at continue is consisting of buff white light gray, light brown, brown, medium hard, hard, densely, slightly argillaceous

Limestone and off white, white gray, medium hard Chalky Limestone beds.

    2.3.4 Mud Logging Activities in F10

Mud logging unit will be spudded at the start of the drilling project containing all standard sensors for controlling the

activities. In geological operation, the required mud logging activites are as below:

     -   Depth controlling
     -   Lag depth calculation through mud Pump GPM and drilling depth
     -  Gas samples gathering, analysis and giving the related reports
     -   Oil show samples gathering and including the results in daily reports.

     -   Colaboration with directional drilling section in order for performing related calculations
     -   Catching the cutting samples, describe the samples and providing graphic logs
     -   Providing daily, weekly and monthly reports according to the company formats
                 -   Preparing the Well Geological Final Report according to the standard formats.

  2.4 General Assumptions

    2.4.1  Well Objective, Target and Total Depth

        Purpose of the Well (Objectives):
         This well will be completed as a fahliyan oil producer at vertical hole.

        Wellbore Quality Evaluation Criteria:
1)  Maximum Deviation and Horizontal Displacement for Vertical Wells

                                                    Bottom                                     Deviation            Depth(m)                                 Hole Shift            DLS（°/30m）                                                (°)
                                                          (m)
            ≤2000               ≤3°              ≤50                      0≤2.25°
          2000~3000             ≤5°              ≤80                       ≤2.50°
          3000~4000             ≤7°              ≤120                      ≤2.75°
          4000~5000             ≤9°              ≤160                      ≤3°

2)  Other requirements for Wellbore Quality

  ⚫  Average borehole diameter enlargement rate less than 15%
  ⚫   All casing/liner are run to designed depth (normally 1-2m above TD/section TD)
  ⚫  No fills at bottom of hole
  ⚫  No junk lost in hole
  ⚫  No abnormal casing wear and casing deformation caused by CONTRACTOR’s malpractice

3)  Well trajectory control key points

   •    Drill upper formation before KOP using Pendulum BHA, low WOB and high RPM, to ensure vertical hole
          drilled.
   •   Control the distance between two holes in one well pad is not less than 2 meters. Single-shot and multi-shot
           will be used to monitor the well trajectory. Take single shot survey at interval of 100m; run multi-shot survey
       each 300m from surface to kick off point. If necessary, survey shall be run more frequently.
   •  Run build up BHA (Motor + MWD) whenever collision risk increases to build up a little inclination (2~4°) and
       bottom hole departure to avoid collision at vertical section if necessary.
   •     If magnetic interference exists, gyroscopic inclinometer shall be used in order to avoid collision.
   •  Amend the planned profile according to the data obtained by electronic multi-shot or single-shot survey in
         vertical hole, and locate the kick off point.

                                   TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY   P | 39

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                   South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                        Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

    Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
   CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

•  MWD/GR will be used in directional drilling to ensure that the actual well path will coincide with the planned
    well trajectory to the extent that it shall not pass through the boundaries of the corridor that surrounds the
    planned hole trajectory in case a corridor has been specified.
•  LWD/GR will be utilized in horizontal drilling in order to controlling well trajectory, especially in Sarvak-3.
•  The downhole motor and MWD shall be used in vertical hole section if necessary.
•   Monitors and analyses the build-up ability of the BHA used in directional/horizontal section. Due to the
    lagged survey data measured by MWD, inclination, azimuth and the buildup rate should be predicted based
   on the instant survey at all times.  If necessary, more surveys should be taken so as to adjust drilling
    parameters and BHA, control the well trajectory within the specified target tolerance strictly.
•   In buildup section, to avoid pipe-stuck, BHA should be designing such way to minimize sliding mode time
    especially in clay formations. To reduce torque in rotating mode and consequently reduce sliding mode time,
    using stabilizer with short gauge and profile.
•  Using circulating sub above MWD is recommended due to sever loss in this area.
•   Adjust the drilling method according to the well survey data, and control the change amplitude of inclination
   and azimuth to ensure the smoothness of the well trajectory.
•   In this section, the torque and drag are relatively higher, torque and drag analysis for each BHA run should
   be performed, and torque and drag reduction measures (for example simplifying the BHA or running cuttings
   bed impeller) are recommended.

 2.4.2  Expected H2S and CO2

     Other risks are taken into consideration in this oilfield such as corrosion gas H2S, CO2 as following.
   The combination of H2S and CO2 is more aggressive than H2S alone, since it possibly causes drill string
   and casing corrosion and drilling crew harmed or poisoned

            F10 | PAD-64

  4. Sequence of Drilling Operation

                                TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY      P | 59

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

  4.1 Rig Move

      ➢  Hold pre-move meeting on location. Discuss contingency plans for:
                       •  Safe arrangements for rig up and rig down.
                       •  Safe standby areas for rig
                       •   In case move have to be suspended.
                       •  Loss of trucks in case of repair.
                       •  Moving at night.
      ➢  Hold pre-move safety meeting. Ensure everyone understands individual responsibilities:
      ➢ Commence rigging down. The ordering of trucks will be done by rig manager.
      ➢ Commence rig move to F10, PAD NO. 64

                       •   Position rig for optimal operations
      ➢  Rig up on cellar
                       •   FinIIF rig up and commence operational procedure for (F10)
                       •   Before leaving previous location, all equipment should be checked.
      ➢  Test all manifold and surface safety valves
                       •  RKB – GLE and Elevation
                       •  Spud date and time.
      ➢ Mud tanks should be empty during move. Fill water tanks and keep as full as possible.

  4.2 Pre spud

   Certificate
      ➢  All rig equipment is on location, properly rigged up and function-tested. Check availability and
                 content of hoisting equipment inspection & maintenance records according to API.

Safety Device
   ➢  Test draw-works crown block saver and record results on daily drilling report; test derrick man escape
         device (Geronimo line) and record time required for test weight on daily drilling report; test rig intercom
         system, especially for Driller-Derrick Man and Driller-Mud Logger communication link.
   ➢  Check that all rig safety equipment, e.g., fire extinguIIFers, eye-wash stations etc., is installed at the proper
          locations as per rig layout diagrams.
   ➢  Verify that substructure is level and foundation adequate to support drilling unit during whole well period
        and that mast is centred above rotary table.
   ➢  Ensure rig alignment with Top Drive System. Make sure rig tongs and rig floor hoisting equipment are
         adequate for handling 8-1/2″ and 9-1/2″ drill collars. Make sure that mechanical tong torque gauges for drill
            pipe/drill collar are properly functioning and have been calibrated; re-calibrated as required.

                                    TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY      P | 60

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

Rig Equipment

➢  Verify that the 26″ tri-cone bit is ready to be picked up, all nozzles installed, and 26″ bit breakers are on the rig
     floor. Make sure single-shot or multi-shot survey tools available for vertical section survey.
➢ Make sure all solids control equipment are correctly installed and have been function-tested with charging
    pressure gauges in place where applicable. Make sure adequate screen inventory for the shale shakers is
    available in the sizes required for the well. Make sure water tanks are completely filled and transfer lines from
    water tanks to mud system are hooked up. Make sure all mud materials and chemicals required to drill 26″ section
     is on location, in sound condition and suitably stored. Make sure that bentonite is pre-hydrated and sufficient
   volume has been built for top section and for well-kill.
➢ Make sure that all mud pumps have been fitted with new consumable parts: liners, pistons, valves, seats etc.
   Make sure that pulsation dampeners are charged with Nitrogen to the proper pre-charge pressure, 30% of
    expected standpipe pressure during drilling of the 26″ hole section; check if sufficient Nitrogen bottles are available
   on location to adjust pulsation dampener pre-charge pressure as required. Make sure that mud pump pop-off
    valves are set at the proper pressure for the liner sizes installed and that the mud discharge lines from the pop-
     off valves to the mud tanks are properly secured. Check that all high-pressure hoses are properly secured with
    safety chains. P/T should be done to high-pressure surface line of circulating system.

Wellhead & BOP
   ➢ Have 2″ line pipe nipple with 2″ ball valve welded to 30″ conductor pipe 0.2m above cellar bottom to drain
        cement from conductor/riser pipe after cementing 20″ surface casing. Verify that pre-installed 30″ conductor
          pipe is vertical and that no angle/bend exists between conductor and 30″ riser pipe.
   ➢ Make sure that adequate supply of 20″ casing is on location, inspected and ready to be run, float shoe
         should already be made up, centralizers should be pre-installed on the pipe-rack prior to spud in the well.
   ➢  Ensure mud logging unit is functioning and equipment are calibrated and tested properly.

Others
  ➢  Verify that the rig’s fuel tanks are full to meet minimum of three days rig consumption available on site.
  ➢ Make sure all waste treatment equipment available and commissioning test is ok.
  ➢  Hold pre-spud meeting with relevant service Company personnel on location. Discuss procedures involved
        throughout 26″ hole section drilling, reporting system and critical safety operation measures.
  ➢ NIDC prepared a pre-spud checklist and distributed it in rigs. The checklist will be performed based on the
       aforementioned checklist. In addition to the checklist, following matters should be checked:

  ➢  Check & observe the alignment of the rig. This check will be performed in four stages; rig levelling, derrick
          verticality, derrick alignment with rotary table, rotary table alignment with conductor pipe. To do this pick up 3
         joints of 9 3/4" DC and check the verticality of the derrick.
  ➢  Mechanical facilities for mud treatment such as:
               1. Desander, Desilter, mud cleaner, proper size of shaker–screens, hoppers, mud guns & mixers,
               should be all available on rig and functioning properly.
               2. All mud tanks, mud transfer lines & connection should be checked by filling them with water & cross
               wellhead circulation.
               3. Mud engineer laboratory should be equipped with a complete set of testing equipment. The
                  calibration should be done under supervision of well site drilling supervisor before starting the
                operation.

               4. Weight indicator, rotary speed gauge, any smart drilling instrumentation, Top Drive, Stroke meter,
               pressure gauges on manifold and in the drilling console, loss / gain gauge and any other gauges
               should be checked and calibrated.
               5. Stand pipe manifold valves & connection must be checked & pressure tested.
               6. All motors should be checked under load prior to start drilling
               7. Check & control mud stock & cement stock every day & if there is any necessity to request mud
                 material, cement or any drilling tool, it should be prepared & requested at proper time.

  ➢  Location Handed Over

   4.3 Drilling

       4.3.1  26” Hole Section, 20” Casing

       General

        After commencing spud-in, 26” hole drilling will be started. The drilling would be anticipated to go on fast.

           Casing Point               20” Casing @ 150m
           Formation                   Aghajari
           Risks & Hole
                                                  Bit balled up           problems
            Deviation control         INCLINOMETER (TOTCO)

              Bit Type                     1-1-1 (1-1-5)
                                        Pre-Hydrated Bentonite mud

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                   South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                         Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

    Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
   CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

    Sequence of Operation

•  Make up 26” BHA as per BHA design. Tag formation at ±14m.
•    Drill ahead formation down to 151m (20” casing point in Aghajari formation) using Hi-vis bentonite pill. Pay
    enough attention to drill straight hole.

    Note:
    In Case of complete loss while drillin 26” hole Cure by pump LCM pill or cement as cement plug.

➢ TD should be adjusted to have 1m of rat hole below casing shoe.

➢ The exact TD will be determined based on casing tally.

➢ Run Inclinometer (Totco) each 50m to evaluate the verticality of the hole.

➢  Ensure that all drilling tools and equipment are on well site and are in serviceable condition.

➢  Ensure that adequate fIIFing tools for this section of hole are available.

➢ Enough Mud Material, LCM and cement should be available on rig site to use in case of needing.

➢  At TD, Circulate and clean the hole. Spot high viscosity pill at bottom, drop Inclinometer (Totco) POOH to
     surface. Check Bit.
➢  Perform hole condition prior to run casing. If observe any indication of drag, run back to bottom. Wash and
    ream to make sure that the hole condition is good.
➢  Perform safety meeting.
➢  R/U casing running equipment.
➢ Run ±150m (Approx. 13 Jts) 20", 94 ppf, K-55, BTC casing with stab-in duplex shoe to bottom as per casing
    program. Casing shoe should be adjusted to have 1m of rat hole and enough stick up (1 m on rig floor). Fill
     casing by non-LCM fresh mud.
  Note:

➢  Take care when running casing not to set down excessive weight if tight hole or ledges are encountered.

➢ Run stinger on DP’s using the “C” Plate & 5” x 20” centralizer. Sting in. M/U & test cementing lines. Circulate
      for one casing annulus volume. While circulating, check the DPs annulus for any return. Use all DPs weight
    on the string if observing any minor leakage through DPs annulus.
➢ Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be done in due time.
     Displace with mud (1-2 bbls Short Displace). Monitor for pressures. Record and report the final pressure,
     Sting out. Check the float. POOH one stand, Circulate to flush/clean drill pipe.
➢  After cement returns to cellar floor, open the 2" nipple on conductor & flush it.
➢ POOH to surface. L/D stinger.
➢ Keep the casing on the elevator until cement surface samples get hard. Check for casing to be center inside
     conductor pipe.

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                   South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                        Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

    Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
   CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

Note:
➢  Based on the results obtained from cementing, decision will be made to perform top jobs.

➢   If there is no cement return to surface, a top job should be performed to  fill the annulus. Use same slurry
    weight as the primary cement slurry. Ensure the availability of adequate X-over for connecting line pipe to
   cement line. The cement should be accelerated with max. (2%) Calcium Chloride BWOC and no other
    additives. Wait for the top job cement to get hard.

➢  Check casing by slack off weight.

➢  Cut conductor pipe. Rough cut landing joint and L/D C.P. and L.J. Dress and bevel 20” suitable to install and
    to weld casing head housing.

➢  Install and weld Casing Head Housing 20 ¾” – 3000 psi on casing and pressure test the sealing of welded
    casing. (Limit pressure to a maximum of 80% of the collapse strength of the casing, recommended
    testing pressure: 400 psi). Outlets must be aligning with cellar stairs.

➢  Before installing the casing head housing, open all the test ports and clean inside it with water and air.

➢  Check the 20 ¾” wear bushing, 20 ¾” Plug Tester and 13-3/8” Casing Slip and seal assembly in CHH before
     installing CHH (on Surface).

➢  N/U 21 ¼”-3000 BOP stack and related lines and valves and test all to 2400psi.
➢  N/U Bell Nipple & flow line
➢  P/U & M/U Next BHA and stand drill pipes to 2211m.
➢   Fill up the following forms and send to drilling office:
             Casing Tally
          BHA reports
             Casing Report
            Cement Report
             Wellhead diagram
             Hole Section Summary for EOWR

    4.3.2  17-1/2” Hole Section, 13-3/8” Casing

    General

Casing Point                    13-3/8” Casing @ ±2210m (5m TVDbelow top of Gurpi FM)

Formation                        Aghajari, Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi
                                   Tight holes, wash & ream (especially in pipe connections), minor
Risks & Hole problems                                 stucks and bit balling, Hole Deviation, Wahsout

 Deviation control               Single shot each 100m, multi shot in 300m

                           CDR/GR/CALI (Open hole)Logging
                          GR/CCL/CBL/VDL (Cased Hole)
 Bit Type                       115, 135,537,517, PDC (M1965)

                                Pre-hydrated Bentonite Mud and KCL-Polymer Mud
                 MW          67-88pcf
 Drilling Fluid
                       PV           5-27
                                     Vis.          40-50
                   WOB       Maximum Available
 Drilling Parameters        RPM         120-180
                    GPM         800-1000

Formation Sampling

Every 10 meters a sample would be to section TD.
NOTE: In addition to this general rule, geologist may decide to take more samples.

Sequence of Operation

➢ Make up 17-1/2” BHA as per BHA Design. RIH, tag top of cement, Drill out cement & shoe and rat
     hole cement. Work on junk sub.
➢  Watch carefully for any indication of abnormal torque, since there might be a possibility of shoe
     being backed off. Report problem immediately.
➢  Continue drilling 3m below shoe. Clean out the rat hole, circulate clean.
➢   Drill ahead formation down to 25m below 20” casing shoe with hi-vis pill.
    Note:
➢   If use stabilizer to drill out shoe track make sure the RPM & WOB are in low as possible until
    the stabilizer is out from shoe Then Cont Drilling 17-1/2” hole section with same BHA.

➢  Circulate and clean hole, POOH to surface and P/U the same bit & S. Stab @ 20m according to
   BHA program, RIH to bottom.

────────────────────────────────────────────
                  South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                       Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells
  Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.        Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
 CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

➢   Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to 750 m. CBU.
➢  Condition trip to 150m, run back and displace mud in hole by 74 pcf KCL/Polymer.
➢  Continue drilling to 850m or End of Bit Life, circ to clean hole, POOH to surface.
➢  P/U new PDC bit and Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to casing point @ ±2211m, 5m TVD
    below top of Gurpi formation meanwhile perform condition trip each 300 m.

    Note: In case of observe Cherty zone (Pabdeh formation), Stop Drilling, circulate to clean

     the hole, POOH to surface and change PDC bit by Tri Cone Bit.

      Note:
   ➢  For this well Single-shot every 100m and multi-shot survey every 300m to Control Well
          Deviation or take gyro shot (5-6 shots each 500-600m)
   ➢  Adjust mud weight according to program and as hole condition dictate.
   ➢  Tight holes, minor stucks and bit balling are hole problems which happen in offset wells.
   ➢ TD should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole for probable fillings, before
          running casing.
   ➢ Choke manifold and choke line must be lined up for SOFT SHUT-IN through pit or burning
             line.
   ➢  Ensure that adequate fIIFing tools for this section are available.
   ➢  Ensure that enough amount and type of LCM are available and keep ready to mix and pump
       LCM Pills to cure losses.
➢  At section TD, circulate, clean hole. Perform wiper trip to shoe and observe well @ shoe. RIH, Check
      for any fill. CBU on bottom. Spot 100 bbls hi-vis pill @ bottom.
➢   If observed any indication of tight hole, run back to TD. W&R to make sure that the hole condition is
    good prior to run Log.
➢  Rig up Wireline logging equipment and run CDR/GR /CAL log as per program, POOH and L/D log
➢  Perform pre-job safety meeting. R/U casing running equipment. Run casing 68#, N-80, BTC with F.
    shoe below first joint & F. collar as per casing program while Circ. BTM’s up at shoe with low GPM.
➢  Rig pump efficiency should be check in trip-tank before cement job.
➢  Casing shoe depth should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole and enough stick
    up (1.5 m) on rig floor.
➢ When casing is at casing setting depth circulate while preparing for cementing M/U & test cementing
      lines. Circulate for 120% of casing annulus volume.
➢ Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be done in
    due time. Displace with mud. Monitor for pressures.
➢ Bump plug with 1500 psi (Max 80% Casing Burst) / 15 min as CIT (In case of not observing bumping
     during CMT job, perform CIT W/1500psi (Max 80% of 13-3/8” Casing Burst)/15min with consider
    mud weight after D/O CMT 7m above F. Shoe also consider MW while perform CIT), Record and
     report the final pressure, Bleed off pressure, check for back flow, if any back flow observed, hold the
     pressure inside casing until the cement get hard.
➢  Hold on casing string weight. Drop casing hanger. Slack off 80% of casing string weight. Test hanger
     to 800-1000 psi pressure. (Open base flange side outlet valves while testing).
➢ Remove the flow line and diverter. Rough cut L.J. Dress and bevel casing neck.
➢  Install Casing head spool 20 ¾”-3000*13 ⅝”-5000, Pack p-seals and pressure test p-seals and
     cavity with maximum 80% of rated casing collapse pressure. (1800 psi)
➢  Ensure that the casing is drifted; cleaned and tallied priors to RIH.
➢   Fill casing by non-LCM fresh mud.

                    South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                          Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells
     Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.        Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
    CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

       4.3.5  Well Flowing

       o  The main objectives of the 96hrs cleanup and well flowing is to displace completion fluid, possible
           mud losses, and flow back the reservoir fluid until achieving the cleanup operation criteria. The
               cleanup operation procedure by rig is categorized as follows:
       o  Rigging up all related-equipment and perform hydro-test
       o  Retrieving barriers (plug, TWV) and landing protection sleeve in TRSSSV nipple
       o  Displacing the wellbore via CT with suitable method and compatible fluid
       o  Flowing the well over 96hrs thru different choke sizes while testing integrity and surface sampling
       o  Retrieving protection sleeve, inject 5 bbl diesel Close TRSSSV, Set BPV in tubing hanger, secure
                 well & release rig.

Note: Detaill Clean Up and Well Flowing Program Will Be Issued in Due Time.

                              TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY      P | 77

         7.2.1 26” Section from Surface to 151 m

                  Fluid Type                                  PHG, Bentonite Mud

                 Hole Size (in)                                          26

 Interval Discussion:
 Interval Discussion:
 The 26-inch section will be drilled to 150 m through Aghajari formation which contains grey Marl and Claystone
 beds and sandy marl by implementing the Bentonite Mud and Hi-Viscous sweeps in a close system.

 This is a large diameter hole, and the drill cuttings removal mechanism will be a combination of high annular velocities
 and hi-viscous sweeps. The viscous sweep is composed of Pre-Hydrated Gel (PHG). Drill water used as main
 drilling fluid should be treated with 0.5 ppb Lime. This will minimize dispersion of Marl, Clay cuttings and lower
 hydration of well bore.

 This hole is to be cleaned as required but as a minimum, the following should be adhered to:
    •  Every single a 20 bbls hi-viscous PHG pill should be pumped to clean the hole.
    •   At connections, the 50 bbls hi-viscous PHG pill should be spotted at bottom to build filter-cake on the bore-
        hole wall, enhance the hole stability and prevent fill up around bit and BHA.
  Availability of low chloride water might be a logistical issue and shortage of proper drill water is to be expected,
  therefore, XC Polymer / CMC pill should be available at the rig site to be used for mixing hi-viscous sweeps. If
  drilling Water consists of more than 5000mg/lit Chloride and total hardness to be less than 200 mg/lit there would
  malfunction of Bentonite and TH to be treated as well, therefore, utilizing Polymers could be an option to avoid
 any delay while drilling. A large volume of fluid will have to be turned over in next section and emphasis will be

                                TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY      P | 87

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                      South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                             Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells
        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.        Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

 placed on pit management and drill water logistics. The engineer should endeavor to have all the pits full of gel
 based hi-viscous mud to avoid ending up in a “catching up” situation.
Important note: its highly recommended to check the drill water quality prior spudding the well by MIS Ahwaz lab
 to ensure the mud can be mixed with no issues due to the bad water quality thus a water sample to be provided
 to MIS by the client.
 Hydration and dispersion of dry clay are greatly affected if the makeup water contains salt or various metallic ions.
Hence, salinity and total hardness should be checked. The chloride concentration should be less than 5000 mg/l.

 At section TD the following procedure shall be adhered to:

    •   Before pulling out of the hole to run casing the hole shall be swept with a 150 bbls PHG viscous pill and
        spotted at least 70 m bottom of open Hole with same PHG viscous pill.

To control mud weight, beside utilizing solid control equipment, a dump and dilute strategy (with Lime treated drill
 water) should be considered.

Potential mud related problems and hazards

Hole cleaning:
 -  Pump hi-viscous mud more frequently and of greater volume.
 -  Increase pump rate.
Down Hole Loss:
 -  Enough suggested volume of LCM pill to be mixed and prepared in advance.
 -   Sufficient Stock of LCM materials should be stored on the rig site as contingency materials.

Mud composition

Main Fluid Composition:        Gel / Bentonite Mud

 Drill water :      Base fluid
 Caustic Soda         :         Alkalinity Control
Soda Ash              :      Hardness control / Alkalinity control
 Bentonite               :        Viscosifier
Mixing Procedure:
 1-    Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water.
 2-   Treat out any hardness greater than 400 ppm with soda ash. (Based on Drill Water Properties)
 3-  Raise the pH to 10 with additions of 0.5 lb/bbl Caustic Soda.
 4-   Treat out any hardness greater than 200 ppm with soda ash. (Based on Drill Water Properties)
 5-  Add 15 – 20 lb/bbl Bentonite and allow hydrating if possible. Processing with a shearing device will improve yield.
 6-  Add Lime to control pH while drilling.
PHG sweep Composition:
 Drill water              :      Base Fluid
 Caustic soda / Lime            :         Alkalinity control
Soda Ash                            :      Hardness control / Alkalinity control
 Bentonite               :        Viscosifier

     will improve yield.

Solid Control Equipment:
Shakers:
       1.   Initially dress all shakers with API 80 screens.

       2.  Each shaker should be dressed just with same mesh size, and it is meaningless to dress a shaker with
           different mesh sizes.
       3. A high-pressure washer (Wash Gun) is required on the shakers for cleaning the screens to avoid screen
          plugging; pressure washer would remove the fine solids that plug the screens and would not increase the
         water ratio in the mud due to the small amount of water that is used for cleaning.

 Interval Discussion

  The 17 ½” section will be drilled from 20” casing shoe from 150 m through Aghajari formation which contains grey
  Marl and Claystone with thin bed of Sandstone by implementing Bentonite Mud and Hi-Viscous PHG sweeps to
  800 m, then well will be displaced with 74 pcf Salt Polymer Mud to casing depth around 2211 m.
  PHG sweeps can be effectively used to clean the hole as per previous section practice in the first part of drilling
   to 800m, as drilling progressed and while drilling with NaCl/KCl polymer mud, polymer Hi-vis pill will be utilized.
   In second parts of fluid, Salt is used primarily as a soluble weight agent. Nevertheless, the increased salinity of
  the system can also provide a certain degree of inhibition against the swelling clays. At about +/-800m Potassium
   chloride at initial 1% will provide an extra inhibition when introduced into the mud however, based on the formation
  behavior can be maintained and increased to 3% which will provide more inhibition against swelling and bit balling
   while drilling sticky formations such as Marl and Clay. Salt also can be used to reduce dispersion and hydration
   of clays.
  For more inhibition it is recommended using of 0.5 ppb PHPA for removing free clay and marl cuttings from the
  mud system. PHPA will be incorporated into a fresh batch of mud preferably from the reserve pits with reduced
  hardness (<300mg/lit). This could be pumped about 25 bbl pill every stand drilled or as per the hole dictates to
  be decide by the mud engineer and company rep.

                                TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY      P | 91

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                          Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells
     Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.        Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
    CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

Its highly recommended to maintain Drilling Detergent in the salt polymer mud from the start at 0.2 ppb in order
to minimize bit balling and accretion while drilling marl formation.

As per our experience in Azadegan oil field, pumping DD brine at the end of each stand drilled is more effective
to reduce the risk of Bit Balling during drilling this interval.
Fluid Loss in spud pill should be maintained at below 3cc/30min, Percentage of KCl should be 5% and also mud
weight should be designed 2 pcf more than active mud weight. The spud mud volume is about 250bbl or to cover
250m of drilled hole.

Recommended sweeps regime from 153 to 800 meters:

Every single a 20 bbls hi-viscous pill should be pumped to clean the hole.
At connections, the 40 bbls hi-viscous pill should be spotted on bottom in order to build filter-cake on the bore-
hole wall, enhance the hole stability and prevent fill up around bit and BHA.

Potential Mud Related Problems and Mitigation Action:
Hole Cleaning:
This is a large diameter hole, and the drill cuttings removal mechanism will be a combination of high annular velocities
and hi-viscous sweeps. The viscous sweep is composed of Pre-Hydrated Gel (PHG) before 800 m. after this depth,
Hi-vis pill would be designed with second interval fluid which is Salt Polymer mud. Viscous pill will be made by
XC-Polymer or CMC HV to clean the hole to casing depth as per well profile.
Hole would be swept by Hi-Vis pills if any sign of cutting accumulation in annulus (e.g., drag, torque or pressure
increase) been observed. The recommended volume is 20-30bbls each time. If situation dictates pills should be
dumped on bottom’s up to prevent excessive viscosity and solid content of mud.
This hole is to be cleaned as required but as a minimum, the following should be adhered to:
Every middle of each single a 15 bbls hi-viscous pill should be pumped to clean the hole.
At connections, the 35 bbls hi-viscous pill should be spotted on bottom to build filter-cake on the bore-hole wall,
enhance the hole stability and prevent fill up around bit and BHA.

At 800 m the following procedure shall be adhered to displace well with Salt/KCl Polymer Mud:
Before displacing, the hole shall be swept with 100 bbls PHG viscous pill then, 50 bbl of 70 pcf Polymer Pill should
be pumped. At the end of displacing job, in one stage all mud in hole would be displaced with Salt Polymer Mud.
In case of using “wastewater”, a pilot test should be carried out by mud engineer to check same with fifty percent
of drill water to prevent any malfunction of bentonite. Otherwise, MIS won’t be responsible about extra using
chemical to adjust/keep mud properties in specified range.

Down hole losses:
There is a potential loss zone, and it is recommended to have LCM pill available before drilling through that zone.
The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses volume. Sufficient stocks of graded LCM should be kept
at location.
In case of seepage losses, the system should be treated system with a finer grade of LCM at 10 – 15 lb/bbl. In
the event of moderate to high rate of well bore loss, prepare pills at different graded concentrations 20 -40 ppb
and spot over thief zone.
The concentration may vary between 20 – 70 lb/bbl. Ensure that the driller displace the LCM pill out of the string
prior to shutting down the mud pumps. In the event of severe losses, cross-linking pills should be pumped to
minimize losses.

                              TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY      P | 92

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                    South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                          Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells
     Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.        Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
    CCA/C/1400/1     TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

Avoid cuttings accumulation in annulus by proper shakers monitoring and sweep regime as unnecessary higher
mud weight and flow rate since can cause higher ECD in the annulus which can result in induce losses.
Mud system can be pre-treated with medium and coarse calcium carbonate prior to reaching the possible loss
zones as background LCM and bridging strategy. Since the material could be easily filtered out over the shakers,
replenishment these products would be required. A typical addition rate can be one 55 lb sack of each size per
hour into the active system unless hole became completely steady and no loss and gain prevails.
Solid & Mud Weight control
The initial weight will be 65 pcf for the section allow time for the cold mud to warm up and shear to avoid excessive
ECD. With this weight the fluid system will be above the collapse pressure of the well bore and provide bore
stability. That is valid unless the hole condition dictates otherwise. Controlling of MW could be done by running
all SCE and dressing the shakers with finest mesh screens & dumping the sand trap and dilution approximately
each 90 m drilled.
After displacing well with Salt Polymer Mud, all Shaker screens should be changed to finer screen and all solid
control equipment should be run to control the LGS. In the higher Mud Weight should be run both centrifuges as
supper clone mode.
Rheology Control
Dilution with fresh mud and/or premixes should be made periodically to ensure good rheological properties and
minimize solids build-up; Rheology should be maintained in specification to minimize the effects of ECD on the
formation. The mud engineer should be monitoring the shakers and ensure sufficient hole cleaning would be
provided by drilling fluids based on the ROP.
Stuck Pipe and Tight Spots
Pipe Lax pill can be spotted around stuck area to free the pipe.
possible tight spots might be experienced due to marl and clay from formation, its recommended to control the
fluid loss recommended range at +/- 800 meters and ensure NaCl brine will be added to help inhibition.
Influx
Monitoring mud properties and report any sudden changes in the chlorides, hardness at BTM’s up.

Bit balling and Hole instability
Bit Balling can occur due to the soft formation that contains clay and is attributed to different factors other than
mud chemistry, such as bit hydraulics. At times, hydraulics is not adequate to clean the bit and stabilizer balling
might occurred. If hydraulics appears to be adequate, then a strategy of pumping cleaning pills (such as Drilling
Detergent and walnut and/or caustic wash pills) might be considered to minimize bit balling.

Its highly recommended to maintain drilling detergent in the mud from the start at 0.2 ppb to minimize bit balling
while drilling marl formation.

Alternatively, with each stand drilled, a 25 bbl of saturated salt/DD pill (2.5%V/V) can be pumped and circulated
out to minimize bit balling and accretion.
Increased concentration of Drilling Detergent can effectively assist tackle or reduce the bit balling. The usage
should be confirmed by company man.

Using defoamers might be necessary to tackle foaming. In case of low ROP pump down fresh brine treated with
caustic soda. Abrasive substances such as mica or nut plug can help better cleaning of the BHA in a turbulent
flow regime.

First Fluid Composition: (150 to 800 m)
        Gel / Bentonite Mud
Drill water                        :      Base fluid
Caustic Soda     :         Alkalinity Control
Soda Ash                        :      Hardness control / Alkalinity control
Bentonite                        :         Viscosifier

Mixing Procedure:
Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water.
 Raise the pH to 10 with additions of 0.5 ppb Caustic Soda.
 Treat out any hardness greater than 200 ppm with Soda Ash. (Based on Drill Water Properties)
Add 15 – 20 lb/bbl Bentonite and allow hydrating as long as possible.

PHG sweep Composition:
Drill water                        :      Base fluid
Caustic soda      :         Alkalinity control
Soda Ash                        :      Hardness control / Alkalinity control
Bentonite                        :        Viscosifier

Recommended Properties for Bentonite Mud:
Funnel Viscosity             :      >70 sec/qt.

Mixing Procedure:
 Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water.
 Treat out any hardness greater than 400 ppm with 1 lb/bbl Soda ash. (Based on Drill Water Properties)
 Raise the pH to 10 with additions of 0.5 lb/bbl Caustic soda.
Add 20–30 lb/bbl Bentonite and allow hydrating as long as possible (at least 4 hours). Processing with shearing
device will improve yield.

Second Fluid Composition: (800 to TD)
KCl/Salt Polymer Mud composition

Drill water                                     :      Base fluid
KCL                                :       Shale Inhibition
XC-Polymer                    :        Viscosifier / Rheology improver
Starch                             :        Fluid loss control
PAC-LV                          :        Viscosity / Fluid loss control
NaCl                               :       weighting agent and inhibition
Barite                              :       weighting agent
D. Detergent                   :         Bit balling reducer
Biocide                            :        Anti Bacteria

KCl Polymer Mud Mixing Procedure:
Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water.
Add KCl at 3 % by weight (10.5 lb/bbl)
Add NaCl 90 lb/bbl
Raise the pH with additions of Caustic Soda. (0.5-1 lb/bbl)

properties by adding sufficient Xanthan Gum or CMC HV to reach the funnel viscosity or high yield point range in
order to clean the hole periodically.

Recommended Properties for Polymer Pill:
Funnel Viscosity             :      > 60 sec/qt.
                  “OR”
Yield Point         :      > 30 lbs/100 sq ft
Mixing Procedure:
 Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water.
 Treat out any hardness greater than 200 ppm with 1 lb/bbl Soda ash. (Based on Drill Water Properties)
 Raise the pH to 10 with additions of 0.5 lb/bbl Caustic soda.
 Adjust weight of Hi-vis pill by adding NaCl and Barite as per client representative order.
 Adjust the viscosity by adding XC-Polymer 2 – 3 ppb as per mentioned properties.
 In case of adjusting the fluid loss value in active system, adding Starch is expected to keep all parameters in
normal range.

Note: The KCl concentration should be adjusted after pumping Hi-Vis pill and during drilling continuously.

Solid Control Equipment:
Shakers:
Initially dress all three shakers with reused API-80 screens. While drilling to keep the mud weight it should be
changed all screens to finer size API 100 and API 140 shaker by shaker and after each one, wait for a while to
see the effect of it on flow condition of shakers to avoid overflow.

After Displacing well with salt/KCl Polymer Mud, all shale shaker screens should be changed based on flow
condition and ROP. It is highly recommended to reduce the cutting retention time to prevent of any screen
plugging and damaging accordingly.
Mud cleaner which shall be dressed in API 325 to remove LGS while running Desilter and Desander.

Note: Each shaker should be dressed just with same API size, and it is meaningless to dress a shaker with
different mesh sizes.

  12 1/4” hole section will be prepared from scratch.
  Fluid loss should be controlled with Starch LV and Pac LV. The rheological properties  will enhance by
  implementing of CMC-HV or Xanthan Gum. KCl and Salt will inhibit the shale swelling and PAC will encapsulate
  the Shale cuttings in this section. Also, high concentration of Chlorite, generated from NaCl addition will cause to
  minimize Water Activity with accordance to the formation water activity in order to create more inhibition while
   drilling Shale layers out.
  Glycol will help the hole instability to prevent any shale swelling by providing of micro emulsion process.

  Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

  Hole instability: Make sure mud properties are in range and mud weight is adequate to keep well bore stability
  and prevent tight hole. KCl and Glycol are shale inhibitors and are consumed in this section to provide a full
  inhibition.

  Lost circulation: The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. Sufficient stocks of graded LCM
  should be kept at location. In case of seepage losses, the system should be treated with fine grade of LCM at
  25 - 30 ppb, for moderate to severe losses, prepare pills at different graded concentrations and spot across the
   thief zone.

  Engineering Comments

  KCl Content: If cuttings integrity and well condition dictate, increase KCl content up to 5 % by weight.

  Losses: surface and sub surface losses to be monitored, prepare LCM pill in advance.

  Density: Drilling fluid density will be controlled by addition of Limestone. Use available solids control equipment
  to reduce low gravity solids build-up, thus reducing any dilution requirements for density control. Mud weight to
  be increased as hole conditions dictate.
  Yield Point: will be adjusted by adding rheology modifier and control the amount of LGS and MBT.

  Fluid loss control, API: The API fluid loss should be kept within specifications using additions of PAC LV and
  Starch LV.

  CEC: To be controlled with solids control equipment and dilution.

  Hole cleaning: Hole should be regularly checked and swept by pumping Hi-Vis pill at the end of each stand
   drilled.

  Solid control: Ensure shakers are running immediately prior to and during all periods of circulation. Shakers to
  be dressed initially with 100/120 mesh screens and after the mud is sheared, finer screens of 140/170 mesh
  should be dressed. Use centrifuges as required to control MW and LGS.

 Dump and dilute strategy: Dilute active system with premixed fluid to maintain volume and mud properties. The
  concentration of this fluid should be somewhat higher than in the initial mix to compensate depletion.

  Bacterial decay: before any pull out of hole, treat mud system with biocide to prevent bacterial decay.

  Foaming: drilling Anhydrite may cause foam due to flocculation of polymer and air trapping. Treat system with
  Soda ash and defoamer to treat it.

Interval Discussions

For drilling 8 ½" interval, a KCl Polymer WBM system will be used and previous mud should be transferred and
weighted up to use in this interval.

The water-based KCL Polymer system provides effective inhibition when drilling reactive shales/clay through
Below mechanism:

Potassium Chloride provides a source of Potassium ions, which are small enough to fit between the clay
platelets without distorting the shale lattice. The Potassium ions are adsorbed on the exchangeable cation
sites in the shale lattice and this holds the platelets together, which helps to minimize clay and shale
hydration, swelling and dispersion. This system is used primarily to drill moderately reactive shales.
Potential Mud Related Problems and Hazards.

Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:
   •   Potential losses
   •   Differential sticking
   •  Bottom Hole temperature

Stuck Pipe: Differential pressure stuck pipe may occur due to the different gradient between formation and mud.
If stuck pipe occur, Pipe Lax spot should be pumped according with standard Company spotting procedures.

Lost circulation: The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. Partial to severe losses are and
sufficient stocks of LCM should be kept on board. In case of seepage losses, the system should be treated with
fine grade of LCM at 10 ppb, for moderate to severe losses, prepare pills at different graded concentrations and
spot across the thief zone.

In order to avoid LCM plugging MWD tools a circulation sub should be installed in the drill string. The MWD
engineer is to advise on the size and concentration of LCM that can be pumped through MWD Tools. Ensure that
the drilling displace the entire LCM pill out of the string prior to shutting down the mud pumps.

Bottom Hole Temperature: Starch HT is recommended to utilize in this section to prevent any fermentation.

    Mixing Procedure:
    Mixing procedure for preparing main mud:
       •    Fill the tanks with the required volume of fresh-water.
       •  Add KCl at 18-26 ppb (3%)
       •  Add NaCl till reaching to total chloride concentration at 180 g/l.
       •   Raise the pH to 9.5-10.5 with additions of caustic soda.
       •   Treat out any hardness greater than 600 mg/l with Soda Ash.
       •  Add Starch HT at 12-14 ppb.
       •  Add PAC-LV at 2 -4 ppb.
       •  Add XC-Polymer at 1 - 2 ppb.
       •  Add Defoamer at required concentration and mix properly.
       •  Add CaCO3 fine to adjust MW at the desired mud weight.

Waste Management General Plan Introduction

The waste water and drilling fluids waste treatment is the main important part of the services that should be
consider more to minimize the waste treatment cost. In addition, by recycling the water phase of the waste,
we reduce the water consumption and recover soluble chemicals. This reduces the cost of material
consumptions and the cost of dumping waste water at site. This process is usually done by using a
chemically mechanically method to separate drilling solids and recycle water. This process is usually done
by using a high-speed centrifuges or filtering equipment and chemical dosing system. This process is very
efficient in drilling site especially in locations that water supply is the main problem. Chemical Precipitation
is the most common technology used to remove the dissolved particles from solutions. The ionic particles
are converted to an insoluble form (particle) by the chemical reaction between the soluble compounds and
the precipitating reagent. The particles formed by this reaction are removed from solution by settling,
filtration or other separation equipment. The unit operations typically required in this technology includes
neutralization, precipitation, coagulation/ flocculation, solids/liquid separation.
Cutting’s treatment is important part of the waste management services which is usually done to solidification
and stabilize and fix the liquid phase movement on the solids. In this treatment, material is added to reduce
free water and possibly reduce or slow potential contaminant release. The treated cuttings will be transferred
to environmentally designated area specified by client.
All related services such as design, supply, execute, supervise and control of waste water and waste mud
treatment plan was performed according to the industry standards. The above-mentioned waste treatment
operations performed under and complied with Iranian Petroleum Standards (IPS) as well as good
engineering practices in oil and exploration industry.

                              TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY      P | 105

Drilling Waste Management Process

Waste Management Services Policy

PDF offers a wide range of waste management services to minimize generated waste and contaminated
discharged cuttings during drilling operations. This helps keep the environment clean and ensures that our
operations are in compliance with DOE (Department of Environment) legislations.
We provide cost-effective and optimum solutions from environmentally acceptable procedures to achieve
zero-discharge plans and improve fluids recovery. The outcome reduces operational costs and enables
recycling.
Wastes are analyzed for various parameters such as chloride, nitrate, phosphate, BOD (Biological Oxygen
Demand), COD (Chemical Oxygen Demand) and  oil content. Other analysis is carried out to monitor
treatment processes on a regular basis during waste generation. Our waste management personnel are fully
trained, experienced and have the competencies in place to provide the best reliable services with
concentrated support and the least liabilities.

                              TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY      P | 106

Drilling Waste Management Steps

Waste Treatment Plan

The drilling waste management plan for drilling operation is based on the major produced wastes during the
drilling operation. Due to the environmental restriction, it is important to monitor the chemical elements not
to be discharged to the ground water.

Seven items have been considered during the drilling to protect the environment as follow:
   ▪   Drilling Fluids Waste
   ▪  Waste Water
   ▪   Oily Waste Water
   ▪  Cement Slurry Waste
   ▪   Drilling Cuttings Waste
   ▪  Water Discharge Area (Waste Pit Design and Protection)
   ▪   Cuttings Discharge Area
According to drilling fluid, drilling waste divided in 2 major category, Waste management plan will be design
for Water Based Mud (WBM) or Oil Based Mud (OBM)

Waste Water Treatment

The waste water and drilling fluids waste treatment is the main important part of the services that should be
consider more to minimize the waste treatment cost. In addition, by recycling the water phase of the waste,
we reduce the water consumption and recover soluble chemicals. This reduces the cost of material
consumptions and the cost of dumping waste water at site.
This process is usually done by using a chemically mechanically method to separate drilling solids and
recycle water. This process is usually done by using a high-speed centrifuges or filtering equipment and
chemical dosing system. This process is very efficient in drilling site especially in locations that water supply
is the main problem.

Drilling Cuttings Treatment

Cuttings Treatment Process

Cutting’s treatment is important part of the waste management services which is usually done to solidification and
stabilize and fix the liquid phase movement on the solids.

Solidification refers to techniques that encapsulate the waste in a monolithic solid of high structural integrity. In this
treatment, material is added to reduce free water and possibly reduce or slow potential contaminant release.
Contaminant migration is restricted by vastly decreasing the surface area exposed to leaching by isolating the wastes
within an impervious capsule.

Stabilization refers to those techniques that reduce the hazard potential of wastes by converting contaminants into
their least soluble, mobile, or toxic form. The physical nature and handling characteristics of the waste are not
necessarily changed by stabilization.

    Flocculation Unit
Chemical Precipitation is the most common technology used to remove the dissolved particles from solutions. The
ionic particles are converted to an insoluble form (particle) by the chemical reaction between the soluble compounds
and the precipitating reagent. The particles formed by this reaction are removed from solution by settling, filtration
or other separation equipment. The unit operations typically required in this technology includes neutralization,
precipitation, coagulation/ flocculation, solids/liquid separation. The flocculation unit is comprising three main parts;

    1-  Chemical Mixing Area
                                                                                  Charge Neutralization
    2-  Chemical Dosing Unit                                                                           Like  charged   particles                                                                                                               repel removal of charge
                                                                                              can enable  particles  to
    3-  Control Unit                                                                          approach close enough to
                                                                                                       coagulate

                                                                  Bridging Flocculation
                                                                             Polymer chains can
                                                                                       "bridge" particles to
                                                                                     create larger masses that
                                                                                                  settle out

Laboratory Test Kits

Spectrophotometry

Spectrophotometry uses photometers that can
measure a light beam's intensity as a function of
its     color     (wavelength)    known    as
spectrophotometers.   Important   features   of
spectrophotometers are spectral bandwidth, (the
range of colors  it can transmit through the test
sample),  and  the  percentage  of  sample-
transmission,  and  the  logarithmic  range  of
sample-absorption and sometimes a percentage
of reflectance measurement.
A spectrophotometer is commonly used for the
measurement  of  transmittance  or  reflectance  of                      Figure 11-Spectrophotometry
solutions,  transparent  or opaque  solids,  such  as
polIIFed glass, or gases

Conductivity Meter

The common  laboratory  conductivity meters
employ  a  potentiometric  method  and  four
electrodes. Often, the electrodes are cylindrical
and arranged concentrically. The electrodes are
usually made of platinum metal. An alternating
current  is applied  to  the outer  pair  of  the
electrodes. The potential between the inner pair
is measured. Conductivity could in principle be
determined using the distance between the
electrodes and  their surface area using the
Ohm’s Law but generally, for accuracy, a calibration is                                                                        Figure 12-Conductivity Meter
employed   using    electrolytes    of   well-known
conductivity.

pH Meter

A pH Meter is a scientific instrument that measures the hydrogen-ion concentration (or
pH) in a solution, indicating  its acidity or alkalinity. The pH meter measures the
difference in electrical potential between a pH electrode and a reference electrode.
Knowledge of pH to greater or lesser accuracy is useful or critical in a great many
situations, including of course chemical laboratory work. pH meters of various types
and quality can be used for soil measurements in agriculture; water quality for water
supply systems, swimming pools, etc.; brewing, industrially or domestically; healthcare,
to ensure that solutions are safe when applied to patients or lethal as storylands and
disinfectants; and many other applications. It has measurements from 0 to 14

Titration Method

Titration, also known as titrimetric, is a common laboratory method
of quantitative chemical analysis that  is used to determine the
unknown concentration  of an  identified analyze. Since volume
measurements play a key role in  titration,  it  is also known as
volumetric analysis. A reagent, called the  titrant or  titration  is
prepared as a standard solution. A known concentration and volume
of titrant reacts with a solution of analyze or titrant to determine
concentration. The volume of titrant reacted is called titration volume

        8. Cement Program

                              TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY   P | 122

────────────────────────────────────────────
────────────────────────────────────────────
                     South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project
                            Drilling & Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

        Contract No.        Co.           Prj.          Dis.       Dep.       Doc.        Rev.        Well No.
      CCA/C/1400/1    TDDC      SAZP     PM      ENG     WDP       02         195(F10)

Summary:
   •  Cement of the well is part of the well integrity barrier envelope. In addition, cement protects the casing
       from corrosion due to potential formation fluids on the outside well.
   •  Cementing in the 17 ½” section returns to surface has historically been a challenge. This is because
        of the formation strength below the 20” surface casing is not sufficient to bring cement all them way to

       the surface.
   •  Cementing the 12 ¼” section is as equally challenging as for the 17 ½” section, due to small limit
      between pore pressure and fracture pressure.
   •  20” surface casing will be set @±151m. Cemented up to surface till return to surface (cellar floor).
   •  13 ⅜" intermediate casing will be set @±2210m. Cemented up to cellar floor. 40% excess on slurries
        for open hole-Casing volume & 20% excess on slurries for casing-casing volume.
   •  9 ⅝" production casing will be set @±3942.5m. It will be cemented up to cellar floor. 40% excess on
        slurries for open hole-Casing volume & 20% excess on slurries for casing-casing volume.
   •  7" liner will be set @ ±4199 mMD. It will be cemented up to liner lap @ ±3862.5 m (80 m overlap on
       production CSG). 50% excess on slurries for open hole-Casing volume & 20% excess on slurries for

       casing-casing volume.

General Procedure:

         Prior to each cement job, the unit will be checked accurately. Fluid ends, engines, etc.

      The lines will have to be flushed with the mixing water. Cementing heads, plugs will be inspected

        before the job.

      The plugs  will be  inserted  into  the cementing head  in presence  of  the  Drilling Operation

        Representative.

      Bumping the plug is MUST.

      The cement density will be checked, using pressurized balance.

      Volume calculations & displacement will be done by & cement engineer.

       Rig pump should be used to displace the cement.

Important: The following program is a sample; therefore,  it should be tested using the field drilling water,

cement sample and additives.

    11.1.2   26" Hole Section, 20” Casing

   151m in Aghajari FM

Important Note:
        1.  The casing shoe must be duplex, stab in, nON-DIFFERENTIAL type.

        3.  Tighten casing with chain before cementing.
